2. Find the critical edges whose removal will disrupt all paths between A and H.

### Step-by-Step Analysis:

- **Node A** connects to **Node B**, **Node E**, and **Node P**.
- **Node H** connects to **Node T**, **Node G**, and **Node C**.
- Traverse through **Node B**, **Node E**, and **Node P** to find paths to **Node H**:

1. **Path via Node B**:
   - **A -> B -> E -> F -> C -> H**

2. **Path via Node E**:
   - **A -> E -> F -> C -> H**
   - **A -> E -> S -> T -> H**
   - **A -> E -> S -> F -> C -> H**

3. **Path via Node P**:
   - **A -> P -> Q -> I -> J -> H**
   - **A -> P -> Q -> I -> J -> R -> H**

4. **Other paths through intermediary nodes**:
   - **A -> E -> S -> F -> T -> H**
   - **A -> E -> N -> O -> T -> H**
   - **A -> E -> N -> S -> T -> H**
   - **A -> E -> F -> T -> H**
   - **A -> E -> F -> Q -> I -> J -> H**

### Cut Set Selection:

To minimize the number of edges to remove while ensuring complete disconnection:
1. Removing **A -> E**: This disrupts all paths involving E.
2. Removing **P -> Q**: This disrupts paths involving P and Q.
3. Removing **J -> H**: This disrupts the direct path from J to H passing through R.

### Conclusion:

The minimum number of edges to remove to completely disconnect the nodes A and H is 3. The edges to remove are:

**Edges to remove: 3, Cut Set: [(A, E), (P, Q), (J, H)].**
